\inf_{\widehat{f}}\sup_{f_{0}\in\mathrm{BV}_{\infty}(L,M)}\mathbb{E}_{f_{0}}\|\widehat{f}-f_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\geq\frac{a^{2}\epsilon^{d}}{4}\inf_{\widehat{\theta}}\max_{\theta\in\Theta}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\rho(\widehat{\theta},\theta)\geq\frac{a^{2}\epsilon^{d}|S|}{16\exp(1)}.

(S.28)

Step 3: Lower bound.

The upshot of Steps 1 and 2 is that the solution to the following constrained
maximization problem yields a lower bound on the minimax risk: letting

s=|S|
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{maximize}\quad

\displaystyle\frac{a^{2}\epsilon^{d}s}{16\exp(1)},

\displaystyle\mathrm{subject~to}\quad

\displaystyle 1\leq s\leq\epsilon^{-d},

\displaystyle as\epsilon^{d-1}\leq\frac{L}{2d},

\displaystyle a\leq M,

\displaystyle\frac{na^{2}\epsilon^{d}}{2}\leq 1.

Setting
a=M,\epsilon=(\frac{2}{a^{2}n})^{1/d}
, and
s=\frac{L}{2da}\epsilon^{-(d-1)}
is feasible for this problem if
2dM(\frac{M^{2}n}{2})^{-\frac{(d-1)}{d}}\leq L\leq 2dM(\frac{M^{2}n}{2})^{1/d}
, and implies that the optimal value is at least

\frac{2^{1/d}}{32\exp(1)d}LM(M^{2}n)^{-1/d}
. This implies the
claim (
30
) upon suitable choices of constants.
∎

D.3
Proof of Lemma
1

In this proof, write
\theta_{0}:=(f_{0}(x_{1}),\ldots,f_{0}(x_{n}))
and

\mathbb{E}_{z|x}[\cdot]=\mathbb{E}[\cdot|x_{1:n}]
.
We will use
D
to represent the modified edge incidence operator
with either clipped edge weights or unit weights; the following analysis,
which uses the scaling factor
\tau_{n}
, applies to both. Let

\mathscr{X}=\mathscr{X}_{1}\cap\mathscr{X}_{2},

(S.29)

with
\mathscr{X}_{1},\mathscr{X}_{2}
as in Section
F
.
By the law of iterated expectation,

\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{n}\|\widehat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\biggr]=\mathbb{E}_{x}\biggl[\mathbb{E}_{z|x}\Bigl[\frac{1}{n}\|\widehat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr]\cdot 1\{x_{1:n}\in\mathscr{X}\}\biggr]+\mathbb{E}_{x}\biggl[\mathbb{E}_{z|x}\Bigl[\frac{1}{n}\|\widehat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr]\cdot 1\{x_{1:n}\not\in\mathscr{X}\}\biggr].

(S.30)

We now upper bound each term on the right hand side separately.

For the first term, we will proceed by comparing the penalty operator

D
to the averaging operator (
S.74
)
and surrogate operator
T
corresponding to the
graph (
S.75
). By construction
x_{1:n}\in\mathscr{X}
implies,
for
(\xi_{k},u_{k})
the
k
th singular value/left singular vector of

T
, that

\displaystyle\lambda

\displaystyle\geq C_{1}\sigma\tau_{n}(\log n)^{1/2+\alpha}

\displaystyle\geq\max\left\{8\max_{\ell}|\mathcal{C}_{\ell}|^{1/2}\Phi_{1}(D,T,A)\cdot\sigma\sqrt{\log 2n^{4}\cdot\sum_{k=2}^{n}\frac{\|u_{k}\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\xi_{k}^{2}}},\Phi_{2}(D,T,A)\cdot\sigma\sqrt{2\log n}\right\},

where the latter inequality follows
from combining
(
S.71
), (
S.72
)
with (
S.76
), (
S.77
)
in the clipped weights case, or
(
S.78
), (
S.79
)
in the unit weights case, for an appropriately chosen
C_{1}
.
We may therefore apply Theorem
S.1

with
D
,
T
, and
A
, which gives

\mathbb{E}_{z|x}\biggl[\frac{1}{n}\|\widehat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\biggr]\cdot 1\{x_{1:n}\in\mathscr{X}\}\leq C\biggl(\frac{\lambda\|D\theta_{0}\|_{1}}{n}+\frac{\log^{\alpha}n}{n}\biggr),

(S.31)

On the other hand, to upper bound the second term
in (
S.30
) we use (
S.70
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{z|x}\Bigl[\frac{1}{n}\|\widehat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr]\cdot 1\{x_{z|x}\not\in\mathscr{X}\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{z|x}\Bigl[\frac{16\|z_{1:n}\|_{2}^{2}}{n}+\frac{2\lambda\|D\theta_{0}\|_{1}}{n}\Bigr]1\{x_{1:n}\not\in\mathscr{X}\}

(S.32)

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl(16+\frac{2\lambda\|D\theta_{0}\|_{1}}{n}\Bigr)1\{x_{1:n}\not\in\mathscr{X}\}.

Substituting (
S.31
)
and (
S.32
)
into (
S.30
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{n}\|\widehat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigl(\frac{\lambda\mathbb{E}\|D\theta_{0}\|_{1}}{n}+\frac{\log^{\alpha}n}{n}+\mathbb{P}(x_{1:n}\not\in\mathscr{X})\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigl(\frac{\lambda\mathbb{E}\|D\theta_{0}\|_{1}}{n}+\frac{\log^{\alpha}n}{n}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=C\Bigl(\frac{\sigma\tau_{n}(\log n)^{1/2+\alpha}\mathbb{E}\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert_{1}}{n}+\frac{\log^{\alpha}n}{n}\Bigr),

(S.33)

with the second inequality following from
Lemma
S.13
, and the equality from the choice of

\lambda=C_{1}\sigma\tau_{n}(\log n)^{1/2+\alpha}
.
∎

D.4
Proof of Lemma
2

We prove the claim (
33
) separately for the unit weights
and clipped weights case (recall that they differ by a scaling factor

\bar{\tau}_{n}
.
We will subsequently abbreviate
f:=f_{0}
and use the notation

\DTV(\;\cdot\;;w^{\varepsilon\leftarrow r})
to denote the

\varepsilon
-neighborhood graph TV, having set
\varepsilon=r
.

D.4.1
Unit weights

Our goal is to upper bound

\mathbb{E}\Big[\DTV\Big(f(x_{1:n});\,\check{w}^{\mathrm{V}}\Big)\Big]=n(n-1)\mathbb{E}\Bigl[|f(x_{1})-f(x_{2})|1\{\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\bar{V}_{1}\cap\bar{V}_{2})>0\}\Bigr].

By conditioning, we can rewrite the expectation above as

p_{\mathrm{max}}^{2}\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}|f(y)-f(x)|\mathbb{P}_{x_{3:n}}\{\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\bar{V}_{x}\cap\bar{V}_{y})>0\}\,dy\,dx,

(S.34)

where
V_{x}=\{z:\|z-x\|_{2}<\|z-x_{i}\|~\forall i=2,3,\ldots,n\}
,
and likewise for
V_{y}
. Note that
V_{x}
and
V_{y}
are random subsets of

\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

We now give an upper bound on the probability that the random cells
\bar{V}_{x}

and
\bar{V}_{y}
intersect on a set of positive Hausdorff measure, by relating
the problem
to uniform concentration of the empirical mass of balls in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. The upper
bound will be crude, in that it may depend on suboptimal multiplicative
constants, but sufficient for our purposes. Define
r(V_{x}):=\sup\{\|z-x\|:z\in V_{x}\}
. Observe that if
\|y-x\|>r(V_{x})+r(V_{y})
, then

\bar{V}_{x}\cap\bar{V}_{y}=\emptyset
, since for any
z\in V_{x}
, by the triangle inequality

\{\|z-y\|\geq\|y-x\|-\|z-x\|>r(V_{y})\}\Longrightarrow\{z\not\in V_{y}\};

therefore

\{\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\bar{V}_{x}\cap\bar{V}_{y})>0\}\Longrightarrow\{\|y-x\|\leq r(V_{x})+r(V_{y})\}.

Now, choose
z\in V_{x}
for which
\|z-x\|=r(V_{x})
. Observe that the ball

B(z,r(V_{x})/2)
must have empirical mass
0
, i.e.,
B(z,r(V_{x})/2)\cap\{x_{3},\ldots,x_{n}\}=\emptyset
(indeed, this same fact must hold for any

r<r(V_{x})
). Therefore,

\mathbb{P}_{x_{3:n}}\{r(V_{x})\geq t\}\leq\mathbb{P}_{x_{3:n}}\Bigl\{\exists z:B(z,t/2)\cap\{x_{3},\ldots,x_{n}\}=\emptyset\Bigr\}.

It follows from Lemma
S.14
that if

t_{n,\delta}=c\bigl(\frac{1}{n}(d\log n+\log(1/\delta)\bigl)^{1/d}<t_{0}
,
where
t_{0}
is a constant not depending on
n,\delta
, then

\mathbb{P}_{x_{3:n}}\{\exists z:B(z,t_{n,\delta}/2)\cap\{x_{3},\ldots,x_{n}\}=\emptyset\}\leq\delta.

Summarizing this reasoning, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x_{3:n}}\{\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\bar{V}_{x}\cap\bar{V}_{y})>0\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{x_{3:n}}\Bigl\{\|y-x\|\leq{r}(V_{x})+{r}(V_{y})\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{x_{3:n}}\Bigl\{\|y-x\|\leq 2{r}(V_{x})\Bigr\}+\mathbb{P}_{x_{3:n}}\Bigl\{\|y-x\|\leq 2{r}(V_{y})\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{x_{3:n}}\Bigl\{\exists z:|B(z,\|x-y\|/4)\cap\{x_{3},\ldots,x_{n}\}|=\emptyset\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\mathbb{P}_{x_{3:n}}\Bigl\{\exists z:|B(z,\|x-y\|/4)\cap\{x_{3},\ldots,x_{n}\}|=\emptyset\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}2,&\quad\textrm{if $\|x-y\|_{2}\leq 2t_{n,\delta}$},\\
2\delta,&\quad\textrm{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Setting
\delta_{n}=n^{-(d+1)/d}
and plugging this back
into (
S.34
), we conclude that if
t_{n,\delta_{n}}<t_{0}
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\DTV\Big(f(x_{1:n});\,\check{w}^{\mathrm{V}}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 2n(n-1)\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}|f(y)-f(x)|\Bigl(1\{\|x-y\|\leq 2t_{n,\delta_{n}}\}+2\delta_{n}\Bigr)\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{DTV}(f;w^{\varepsilon\leftarrow t_{n,\delta}})]+2n^{1-1/d}\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}|f(y)-f(x)|\,dy\,dx.

(S.35)

Note that since
\lim_{n\to\infty}t_{n,\delta_{n}}=0
, the condition

t_{n,\delta_{n}}<t_{0}
will automatically be satisfied for all
n
sufficiently
large.

We now conclude the proof by upper bounding each term in (
S.35
).
The first term refers to the expected
\varepsilon
-neighborhood graph total
variation of
f
when
\varepsilon=t_{n,\delta_{n}}
, and
by (
S.39
) satisfies

\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{DTV}_{n,t_{n,\delta}}(f)]\leq Cn^{2}(t_{n,\delta_{n}})^{d+1}\mathrm{TV}(f;\Omega)\leq Cn^{1-1/d}(\log n)^{(d+1)/d}\mathrm{TV}(f;\Omega).

The second term above can be upper bounded using a Poincaré inequality for

\mathrm{BV}(\Omega)
functions, i.e.,

\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}|f(y)-f(x)|\,dy\,dx\leq 2\int_{\Omega}|f(x)-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{f}(x)|\,dx\leq C\mathrm{TV}(f;\Omega).

Plugging these upper bounds back into (
S.35
) yields the claimed
result (
33
) in the unit weights case.
∎

D.4.2
Clipped weights

We now show (
33
) using clipped weights. Our goal is to
upper bound

\mathbb{E}\Big[\DTV\Big(f(x_{1:n});\,\tilde{w}^{\mathrm{V}}\Big)\Big]=n(n-1)\mathbb{E}\Bigl[|f(x_{1})-f(x_{2})|\max\{c_{0}n^{-(d-1)/d}{1}\{\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\bar{V}_{1}\cap\bar{V}_{2})>0\},\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\bar{V}_{1}\cap\bar{V}_{2})\}\Bigr].

By conditioning, we may rewrite the expectation above as

p_{\mathrm{max}}^{2}\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}|f(y)-f(x)|\mathbb{E}_{x_{3:n}}\left[\max\{c_{0}n^{-(d-1)/d}{1}\{\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\bar{V}_{x}\cap\bar{V}_{y})>0\},\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\bar{V}_{x}\cap\bar{V}_{y})\}\right]\,dy\,dx,

(S.36)

where
V_{x}=\{z:\|z-x\|_{2}<\|z-x_{i}\|~\forall i=2,3,\ldots,n\}
,
and likewise for
V_{y}
. Note that
V_{x}
and
V_{y}
are random subsets of

\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We now focus on controlling the inner expectation
of (
S.36
). Upper bound the maximum of two positive
functions with their sum to obtain,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{3:n}\Big[\max\{c_{0}n^{-(d-1)/d}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1}\{\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\bar{V}_{x}\cap\bar{V}_{y})>0\},\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\bar{V}_{x}\cap\bar{V}_{y})\}\Big]

(S.37)

\displaystyle\leq c_{0}n^{-(d-1)/d}\mathbb{P}\{\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\bar{V}_{x}\cap\bar{V}_{y})>0\}+\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\bar{V}_{x}\cap\bar{V}_{y})\right].

We recognize the first term on the RHS
of (
S.37
) as having already been analyzed in
the unit weights case; we now focus on the second term. The latter
“Voronoi kernel” term may be rewritten,

\mathbb{E}_{x_{3:n}}\left[\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\bar{V}_{x}\cap\bar{V}_{y})\right]=\int_{L\cap\Omega}(1-p_{x}(z))^{n-2}dz,

where
L=\{z:\lVert x-z\rVert=\lVert y-z\rVert\}
and

p_{x}(z)=P(B(z,\lVert x-z\rVert))
. Observe by
Assumption
Assumption A1
that

p_{x}(z)\geq p_{\mathrm{min}}\mu_{d}\lVert x-z\rVert^{d}
, and therefore

\int_{L\cap\Omega}(1-p_{x}(z))^{n-2}\leq\exp(-cn\lVert x-z\rVert^{d}),

for some
c>0
. Apply Lemma
S.19
with
a=2
to therefore
bound,

\mathbb{E}_{x_{3:n}}\left[\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\bar{V}_{x}\cap\bar{V}_{y})\right]\leq C_{1}\left(\frac{{1}\{\lVert x-y\rVert\leq C_{2}(\log n/n)^{1/d}\}}{n^{(d-1)/d}}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right),

(S.38)

for constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
.
Substitute (
S.38
)
into (
S.37
)
and (
S.36
) to obtain,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[

\displaystyle\DTV\left(f(x_{1:n});\,\tilde{w}\right)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq p_{\mathrm{max}}^{2}n^{2}\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}|f(y)-f(x)|\Bigg(c_{0}n^{-(d-1)/d}\mathbb{P}_{3:n}\{\mathcal{H}(\bar{V}_{x}\cap\bar{V}_{y})>0\}

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt+C_{1}\frac{{1}\{\lVert x-y\rVert\leq C_{2}(\log n/n)^{1/d}\}}{n^{(d-1)/d}}+\frac{C_{1}}{n^{2}}\Bigg)\;dy\;dx

\displaystyle\leq p_{\mathrm{max}}^{2}c_{0}n^{-(d-1)/d}\mathbb{E}\left[\DTV(f(x_{1:n});\check{w}^{\mathrm{V}})\right]+p_{\mathrm{max}}^{2}C_{1}n^{-(d-1)/d}\mathbb{E}\left[\DTV(f(x_{1:n});w^{\varepsilon\leftarrow C_{2}(\log n/n)^{1/d}})\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 256.0748pt+p_{\mathrm{max}}^{2}C_{1}\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}|f(y)-f(x)|\;dy\;dx

\displaystyle=T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}.

We bound each of the terms above in turn. The first term appeals
to (
33
) in the unit weights case, which we have already
proved.

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle=p_{\mathrm{max}}^{2}c_{0}n^{-(d-1)/d}\mathbb{E}\left[\DTV(f(x_{1:n});\check{w}^{\mathrm{V}})\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}n^{-(d-1)/d}n^{(d-1)/d}(\log n)^{1+1/d}\TV(f)

\displaystyle=C_{3}(\log n)^{1+1/d}\TV(f).

The second term refers to the expected
\varepsilon
-neighborhood
graph total variation of
f
when
\varepsilon=C_{2}(\log n/n)^{1/d}
,
which by (
S.39
) satisfies,

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle=p_{\mathrm{max}}^{2}C_{1}n^{-(d-1)/d}\mathbb{E}\left[\DTV\Big(f(x_{1:n});w^{\varepsilon\leftarrow C_{2}(\log n/n)^{1/d}}\Big)\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}n^{-(d-1)/d}n^{2}(\log n/n)^{(d+1)/d}\TV(f)

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}(\log n)^{1+1/d}\TV(f).

The third term can be controlled via the Poincaré inequality,

\displaystyle T_{3}

\displaystyle=p_{\mathrm{max}}^{2}C_{1}\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}|f(y)-\bar{f}+\bar{f}-f(x)|\;dy\;dx

\displaystyle\leq C_{5}\int_{\Omega}|f(x)-\bar{f}|\;dx

\displaystyle\leq C_{5}\TV(f),

where
\bar{f}:=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Omega}f
.
∎

D.4.3
\varepsilon
-neighborhood and kNN expected discrete TV

Lemma S.6
.

Under Assumption
Assumption A1
, there exist constants

c,C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that for all sufficiently large
n
and

f_{0}\in\BV(\Omega)
,

•

The
\varepsilon
-neighborhood graph total variation, for
any
\varepsilon>0
, satisfies

\mathbb{E}\Big[\DTV\Big(f_{0}(x_{1:n};\;w^{\varepsilon})\Big)\Big]\leq C_{1}n^{2}\varepsilon^{d+1}\TV(f_{0}).

(S.39)

•

The
k
-nearest neighbors graph total variation,
for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, satisfies

\mathbb{E}\Big[\DTV\Big(f_{0}(x_{1:n};\;w^{k})\Big)\Big]\leq C_{2}\left(n^{1-1/d}k^{(d+1)/d}+n^{2}\exp(-ck)\right)\TV(f_{0}).

(S.40)

Proof.

\varepsilon
-neighborhood expected discrete TV.

This follows the proof of Lemma 1 in
Green et al. 2021a
, with two
adaptations to move from Sobolev
H^{2}(\Omega)
to the
\BV(\Omega)
: we deal in
absolute differences rather than squared differences, and an approximation
argument is invoked at the end to account for the existence of non-weakly
differentiable functions in
\BV(\Omega)
.

Begin by rewriting,

\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}|f(x_{i})-f(x_{j})|\cdot{1}\{\lVert x_{i}-x_{j}\rVert\leq\varepsilon\}\right]=\frac{n(n-1)}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[|f(X^{\prime})-f(X)|K\left(\frac{\lVert X^{\prime}-X\rVert}{\varepsilon}\right)\right],

(S.41)

where
X
and
X^{\prime}
are random variables independently drawn from
P

following Assumption
Assumption A1
and

K(t)={1}\{t\leq 1\}
. Now,
take
\Omega^{\prime}
to be an arbitrary bounded open set such that

B(x,c_{0})\subseteq\Omega^{\prime}
for all
x\in\Omega
.

For the remainder of this proof, we assume that (i)
f\in BV(\Omega^{\prime})
and
(ii)
\lVert f\rVert_{BV(\Omega^{\prime})}\leq C^{\prime}\lVert f\rVert_{BV(\Omega)}
for some
constant
C^{\prime}
independent of
f
. These conditions are guaranteed by the
Extension Theorem
(
Evans 2010
; Section 5.4 Theorem 1), which promises an
extension operator
E:W^{1,p}(\Omega)\rightarrow W^{1,p}(\Omega)
(take

p=1
and the BV case is established through an approximation argument). We
also assume that
f\in C^{\infty}(\Omega)
, which is addressed through via an
approximation argument at the end. Since
f\in C^{\infty}(\Omega)
, we may
rewrite a difference in terms of an integrated derivative:

f(x^{\prime})-f(x)=\int_{0}^{1}\nabla f(x+t(x^{\prime}-x))^{\top}(x^{\prime}-x)dx.

(S.42)

It follows that

\mathbb{E}\left[|f(X^{\prime})-f(X)|K\left(\frac{\lVert X^{\prime}-X\rVert}{\varepsilon}\right)\leq p_{\text{max}}^{2}\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}|f(x^{\prime})-f(x)|K\left(\frac{\lVert x^{\prime}-x\rVert}{\varepsilon}\right)dx^{\prime}dx\right],

(S.43)

and the final step is to bound the double integral. We have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}

\displaystyle|f(x^{\prime})-f(x)|K\left(\frac{\lVert x^{\prime}-x\rVert}{\varepsilon}\right)dx^{\prime}dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}\Big|\int_{0}^{1}\nabla f(x+t(x^{\prime}-x))^{\top}(x^{\prime}-x)dt\Big|K\left(\frac{\lVert x^{\prime}-x\rVert}{\varepsilon}\right)dx^{\prime}dx

(FTC)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}\int_{0}^{1}|\nabla f(x+t(x^{\prime}-x))^{\top}(x^{\prime}-x)|K\left(\frac{\lVert x^{\prime}-x\rVert}{\varepsilon}\right)dtdx^{\prime}dx

(Jensen)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\int_{B(0,1)}\int_{0}^{1}|\nabla f(x+t\varepsilon z)^{\top}(\varepsilon z)|K(\lVert z\rVert)\varepsilon^{d}dtdzdx

(
z=(x^{\prime}-x)/\varepsilon
)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{d+1}\int_{\Omega}\int_{B(0,1)}\int_{0}^{1}|\nabla f(x+t\varepsilon z)^{\top}z|K(\lVert z\rVert)dtdzdx

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{d+1}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}\int_{B(0,1)}\int_{0}^{1}|\nabla f(\tilde{x})^{\top}z|K(\lVert z\rVert)dtdzd\tilde{x}

\displaystyle\text{($\tilde{x}=x+t\varepsilon z$)}.

Next, we apply the Cauchy-Schwarz to
|\nabla f(\tilde{x})^{\top}z|
to obtain,

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla f(\tilde{x})^{\top}z|K(\lVert z\rVert)dz

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B(0,1)}\lVert\nabla f(\tilde{x})\rVert\lVert z\rVert K(\lVert z\rVert)dz

\displaystyle=\lVert\nabla f(\tilde{x})\rVert\int_{(B(0,1)}\lVert z\rVert K(\lVert z\rVert)dz

\displaystyle=C_{d}\lVert\nabla f(\tilde{x})\rVert

Substituting back in to the previous derivation, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}|f(x^{\prime})-f(x)|K\left(\frac{\lVert x^{\prime}-x\rVert}{\varepsilon}\right)dx^{\prime}dx

\displaystyle\leq C_{d}\varepsilon^{d+1}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{1}\lVert\nabla f(\tilde{x})\rVert_{1}dtd\tilde{x}

\displaystyle=C_{d}\varepsilon^{d+1}\lVert Df\rVert(\Omega^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq C_{d}C^{\prime}\varepsilon^{d+1}\lVert Df\rVert(\Omega)

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}|f(x_{i})-f(x_{j})|\cdot{1}\{\lVert x_{i}-x_{j}\rVert\leq\varepsilon\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n(n-1)}{2}p_{\text{max}}^{2}C_{d}C^{\prime}\varepsilon^{d+1}\lVert Df\rVert(\Omega)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}n^{2}\varepsilon^{d+1}\lVert Df\rVert(\Omega)

Finally, we provide an approximation argument to justify the assumption that

f\in C^{1}(\Omega^{\prime})
. For a function
f\in\BV(\Omega^{\prime})
,
we may construct a sequence of functions
f_{k}\in C^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})
via
mollification such that
f_{k}\rightarrow f

\mu
-a.e. (specifically, at all
Lebesgue points) and
\lVert Df_{k}\rVert(\Omega^{\prime})\rightarrow\lVert Df\rVert(\Omega^{\prime})
as
k\rightarrow\infty

(
Evans and Gariepy 2015
; Theorems 4.1 & 5.3). Via an application of
Fatou’s lemma, we find that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}|f(x_{i})-f(x_{j})|

\displaystyle\cdot{1}\{\lVert x_{i}-x_{j}\rVert\leq\varepsilon\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}|\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}f_{k}(x_{i})-f_{k}(x_{j})|\cdot{1}\{\lVert x_{i}-x_{j}\rVert\leq\varepsilon\}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\liminf_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}|f_{k}(x_{i})-f_{k}(x_{j})|\cdot{1}\{\lVert x_{i}-x_{j}\rVert\leq\varepsilon\}\right]

(Continuity)

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{k\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}|f_{k}(x_{i})-f_{k}(x_{j})|\cdot{1}\{\lVert x_{i}-x_{j}\rVert\leq\varepsilon\}\right]

(Fatou’s lemma)

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{k\rightarrow\infty}Cn^{2}\varepsilon^{d+1}\lVert Df_{k}\rVert(\Omega)

\displaystyle=Cn^{2}\varepsilon^{d+1}\lVert Df\rVert(\Omega)

k
-nearest neighbors expected discrete TV.

Let
\varepsilon_{k}(x):=\|x-x_{(k)}(x)\|_{2}
and
\varepsilon_{k}(x,y)=\max\{\varepsilon_{k}(x),\varepsilon_{k}(y)\}
be data-dependent radii. Notice
that

\mathrm{DTV}_{n,k}(f)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}|f(x_{i})-f(x_{j})|\cdot 1\bigl\{\|x_{i}-x_{j}\|\leq\varepsilon_{k}(x_{i},x_{j})\bigr\}.

By linearity of expectation and conditioning, the expected
k
-nearest
neighbor TV can be written as a double integral,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\DTV(f;w^{k})]

\displaystyle=n(n-1)\mathbb{E}\Bigl[|f(x_{i})-f(x_{j})|~1\bigl\{\|x_{i}-x_{j}\|\leq\varepsilon_{k}(x_{i},x_{j})\bigr\}\Bigr]

\displaystyle=n(n-1)\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathbb{E}\Bigl[|f(x_{i})-f(x_{j})|~1\bigl\{\|x_{i}-x_{j}\|\leq\varepsilon_{k}(x_{i},x_{j})\bigr\}|x_{i},x_{j}\Bigr]\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq n(n-1)\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}|f(y)-f(x)|~\mathbb{P}\bigl\{\|x-y\|\leq\varepsilon_{k}(x,y)\bigr\}\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\leq n(n-1)\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}|f(y)-f(x)|~\Bigl(\mathbb{P}\bigl\{\|x-y\|\leq\varepsilon_{k}(x)\bigr\}+\mathbb{P}\bigl\{\|x-y\|\leq\varepsilon_{k}(x)\bigr\}\Bigr)\,dx\,dy

(The first inequality above is nearly an equality for large
n
, and the second
inequality follows by a union bound.)

We now derive an upper bound

\mathbb{P}\bigl\{\|x-y\|\leq\varepsilon_{k}(x)\bigr\}
. First, observe that
the event
\|x-y\|\leq\varepsilon_{k}(x)
is equivalent to

|B(x,\|y-x\|)\cap x_{1:n}|<k
. Suppose

\|y-x\|\geq C(k/n)^{1/d}
for
C=(\frac{2d}{p_{\mathrm{min}}\mu_{d}})^{1/d}
. Then

p_{k}(x,y):=P(B(x,\|y-x\|))\geq\frac{p_{\mathrm{min}}}{2d}\mu_{d}\|y-x\|^{d}\geq\frac{2k}{n},

and applying standard concentration bounds (Bernstein’s inequality) to the
tails of a binomial distribution, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\biggl\{|B(x,\|y-x\|)\cap x_{1:n}|<k\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\biggl\{|B(x,\|y-x\|)\cap x_{1:n}|-np_{k}(x,y)<k-np_{k}(x,y)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\biggl(-\frac{c(np_{k}(x,y)-k)^{2}}{np_{k}(x,y)+|np_{k}(x,y)-k|}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-ck).

Otherwise if
\|y-x\|<C(k/n)^{1/d}
, we use the trivial upper bound
1
on
the probability of an event. To summarize, we have shown

\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|x-y\|\leq\varepsilon_{k}(x)\bigr)\leq\begin{cases}1,&\quad\textrm{if $\|x-y\|<C(k/n)^{1/d}$,}\\
\exp(-ck),&\quad\textrm{otherwise}.\end{cases}

It follows from (
S.40
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\DTV(f;w^{k})]

\displaystyle\leq 2n^{2}\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}|f(y)-f(x)|~\Bigl(\bigl(1\{\|x-y\|<C(k/n)^{1/d}\}\bigr)+\exp(-ck)\Bigr)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\leq C\bigl(\mathbb{E}[\DTV(f;w^{\varepsilon\leftarrow C(k/n)^{1/d}})]+n^{2}\exp(-ck)\mathrm{TV}(f,\Omega)\bigr);

(S.44)

the first term on the right hand side of the second inequality is the expected

\varepsilon
-neighborhood graph TV of
f
, with radius
C(k/n)^{1/d}
, while
the second term is obtained from the Poincaré inequality

\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}|f(y)-f(x)|\,dy\,dx=\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}\biggl|f(y)-\bar{f}+\bar{f}-f(x)\biggr|\,dy\,dx\leq C\bigl(\mathrm{TV}(f;\Omega)\bigr),

(S.45)

where
\bar{f}=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\Omega}f(x)\,dx
is the average of
f
over

\Omega
.
The claimed upper bound (
S.40
) follows from applying
inequality (
S.39
), with
\varepsilon=C(k/n)^{1/d}
,
to (
S.44
).
∎

D.5
Proof of Lemma
3

Recall that
\lVert g\rVert_{L^{2}(P)}\leq p_{\mathrm{max}}\lVert g\rVert_{L^{2}(\mu)}

for any
g\in L^{2}(\mu)
and
note that
\|\bar{f_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mu)}\leq M
with probability one. By
Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\bar{f_{0}}-f_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\|\bar{f_{0}}-f_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mu)}\cdot\|\bar{f_{0}}-f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mu)}\Bigr]

(S.46)

\displaystyle\leq 2M~\mathbb{E}\|\bar{f_{0}}-f_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mu)},

and the problem is reduced to upper bounding the expected
L^{1}(\mu)
loss of

\bar{f_{0}}
. By Fubini’s Theorem we may exchange expectation with integral, giving

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\bar{f_{0}}-f_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mu)}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\mathbb{E}|\bar{f_{0}}(x)-f_{0}(x)|\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}|f_{0}(y)-f_{0}(x)|p_{x}^{(1)}(y)\,dy\,dx,

(S.47)

where
p_{x}^{(1)}(\cdot)
is the density of
x_{(1)}(x)
. We now give a closed
form expression for this density, before proceeding to lower
bound (
D.5
).

Closed-form expression for
p_{x}^{(1)}
.

Suppose
P
satisfies Assumption
Assumption A1
.
For any
y\in\Omega
and

0<r<\mathrm{dist}(y,\partial\Omega)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl\{x_{(1)}(x)\in B(y,r)\bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq n~\mathbb{P}\bigl\{x_{1}\in B(y,r)\bigr\}\bigl(\mathbb{P}\{x_{2}\not\in B(x,\lVert y-x\rVert\}\bigr)^{(n-1)}

\displaystyle\leq np_{\mathrm{max}}\mu\bigl(B(y,r)\bigr)\Bigl(1-P\bigl(B(x,\|y-x\|)\bigr)\Bigr)^{(n-1)}.

Taking limits as
r\to 0
gives

p_{x}^{(1)}(y)=\lim_{r\to 0}\frac{\mathbb{P}\bigl\{x_{(1)}(x)\in B(y,r)\bigr\}}{\mu(B(y,r))}=np_{\mathrm{max}}\Bigl(1-P\bigl(B(x,\|y-x\|)\bigr)\Bigr)^{(n-1)}.

Upper bound on (
D.5
).

There exists a constant
C_{d}
such that for all
x,y\in\Omega
,

P\bigl(B(x,\|y-x\|)\bigr)\geq\frac{p_{\mathrm{min}}}{C_{d}}\mu(B(x,\|y-x\|))=\frac{p_{\mathrm{min}}\mu_{d}}{C_{d}}\|y-x\|^{d}.

This implies an upper bound on the density of
x_{(1)}(x)
,

\displaystyle p_{x}^{(1)}(y)

\displaystyle\leq n\biggl(1-\frac{p_{\mathrm{min}}\mu_{d}}{C_{d}}\|y-x\|^{d}\biggr)^{(n-1)}

\displaystyle\leq n\exp\biggl(-\frac{p_{\mathrm{min}}\mu_{d}}{C_{d}}\Bigl(\frac{\|y-x\|}{n^{-1/d}}\Bigr)^{d}\biggr),

where we have used the inequality
(1-x)^{n}\leq\exp(-nx)
for
|x|\leq 1
. Using the inequality, valid for all monotone non-increasing functions

g:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
, that
g(t)\leq 1\{t\leq t_{0}\}g(0)+g(t_{0})
,
we further conclude that

p_{x}^{(1)}(y)\leq n1\{\|y-x\|\leq\varepsilon_{n}^{(1)}\}+\frac{1}{n},

for
\varepsilon_{n}^{(1)}:=(\frac{2C_{d}}{p_{\mathrm{min}}\mu_{d}}(\log n/n))^{1/d}
.
Plugging back into (
D.5
), we see that the expected

L^{1}(\mu)
error is upper bounded by the expected discrete TV of a
neighborhood graph with particular kernel and radius, plus a remainder term.
Specifically,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\bar{f_{0}}-f_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mu)}

\displaystyle\leq n\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}|f_{0}(y)-f_{0}(x)|1\{\|y-x\|\leq\varepsilon_{n}^{(1)}\}\,dy\,dx+\frac{1}{n}\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}|f_{0}(y)-f_{0}(x)|\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\leq n\int_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}|f_{0}(y)-f_{0}(x)|1\{\|y-x\|\leq\varepsilon_{n}^{(1)}\}\,dy\,dx+\frac{C~\mathrm{TV}(f_{0};\Omega)}{n}

(S.48)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{E}[\DTV(f_{0};w^{\varepsilon\leftarrow\varepsilon_{n}^{(1)}}))]+\frac{C~\TV(f_{0};\Omega)}{n},

where (
S.48
) above follows from the Poincaré
inequality (
S.45
). We can therefore
apply (
S.39
), which upper bounds the expected

\varepsilon
-neighborhood graph TV, and conclude that

\mathbb{E}\|\bar{f_{0}}-f_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mu)}\leq C\biggl(\frac{(\log n)^{1+1/d}}{n^{1/d}}+\frac{1}{n}\biggr)\mathrm{TV}(f_{0};\Omega)\leq C\biggl(\frac{L(\log n)^{1+1/d}}{n^{1/d}}\biggr).

Inserting this upper bound into (
S.46
) completes the proof of
Lemma
3
.
∎

D.6
Proof of Theorem
4

The analysis of the
\varepsilon
-neighborhood and kNN TV denoising estimators
proceeds identically, so we consider them together. Henceforth let
D

denote the penalty operator for either estimator and
\hat{f}
denote their
1NN extrapolants. Follow the proof of Theorem
2
(given in
Appendix
D.1
) to decompose the
L^{2}(P)
error for some

C>0
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\lVert\hat{f}-f_{0}\rVert_{L^{2}(P)}^{2}\right]\leq C\left(\frac{\lambda\log n\;\mathbb{E}\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert}{n}+\frac{(\log n)^{1+\alpha}}{n}+\frac{LM(\log n)^{1+1/d}}{n^{1/d}}\right),

(S.49)

where we have applied Lemma
3
which controls
the 1NN extrapolation error. Lemma
S.6
provides that
under the standard assumptions, there exist constants
C_{1},C_{1}^{\prime}>0

such that for all sufficiently large
n
and
\theta_{0}=f_{0}(x_{1:n})
,

f_{0}\in\BV(\Omega)
,

•

setting
\varepsilon=c_{1}(\log^{\alpha}n/n)^{1/d}
,

\mathbb{E}\lVert D^{\varepsilon}\theta_{0}\rVert_{1}\leq C_{1}n^{(d-1)/d}(\log n)^{\alpha+\alpha/d}\TV(f_{0});

(S.50)

•

setting
k=c_{1}^{\prime}(\log n)^{3}
,

\mathbb{E}\lVert D^{k}\theta_{0}\rVert_{1}\leq C_{1}^{\prime}n^{(d-1)/d}(\log n)^{3+3/d}\TV(f_{0}).

(S.51)

Take these values of
\varepsilon,k
and

\lambda=c\sigma(\log n)^{1/2-\alpha}
,
c=c_{2},c_{2}^{\prime}
, and substitute
(
S.50
), (
S.51
)
into (
S.49
) to obtain the claim.
∎

Note that the
L^{2}(P_{n})
in-sample error may be obtained similarly, beginning
with an analysis identical to that of Lemma
1

to obtain the preliminary upper bound,

\mathbb{E}\left[\lVert\hat{f}-f_{0}\rVert_{L^{2}(P_{n})}^{2}\right]\leq C\left(\frac{\lambda\;\mathbb{E}\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert}{n}+\frac{(\log n)^{1+\alpha}}{n}\right).

D.7
Proof of Theorem
5

In this section we prove the upper bound (
39
). The proof
is comprised of several steps and we start by giving a high-level summary.

•

We begin in Section
D.7.1
by formalizing the
estimator
\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{wave}}
alluded to in
Theorem
5
, based on hard thresholding of Haar
wavelet empirical coefficients.

•

Section
D.7.2
reviews wavelet coefficient decay
of
\mathrm{BV}(\Omega)
and
L^{\infty}(\Omega)
functions. These rates of
decay imply that the wavelet coefficients of
f_{0}\in\mathrm{BV}_{\infty}(L,M)
must belong to the normed balls in a pair of
Besov bodies, defined formally in (
S.58
). Besov bodies
are sequence-based spaces that reflect the wavelet coefficient decay of
functions in Besov spaces.

•

Section
D.7.3
gives a deterministic
upper bound on the squared-
\ell^{2}
error of thresholding wavelet
coefficients when the population-level coefficients belong to intersections
of Besov bodies. This deterministic upper bound is based on analyzing two
functionals—a modulus of continuity (
S.101
) and
the tail width (
S.102
)— in the spirit
of
(
Donoho et al. 1995
)
; the difference is that we are
considering
intersections
of Besov bodies.

•

The aforementioned modulus of continuity measures the size of the

\ell^{2}
-norm
\|\theta-\theta^{\prime}\|_{2}
relative to
\ell^{\infty}
-norm

\|\theta-\theta^{\prime}\|_{\infty}
. In
Section
D.7.4
, we give an upper bound on the

\ell^{\infty}
norm of the difference between sample and population-level
wavelet coefficients.

•

Finally, in Section
D.7.5
we combine
the results of Sections
D.7.3

and
D.7.4
to establish upper bounds on the
expected squared-
\ell^{2}
error of hard thresholding sample wavelet
coefficients. The same upper bound will apply to the expected
squared-
L^{2}(\Omega)
error of
\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{wave}}
, by Parseval’s
theorem.

D.7.1
Step 1: Hard-thresholding of wavelet coefficients

To define the estimator
\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{wave}}
that achieves the upper bound
in (
39
), we first review the definition of tensor product
Haar wavelets.

Definition 1

(Haar wavelet)
.

The
Haar wavelet

\psi:(0,1)\to\mathbb{R}
is defined by

\psi(x):=1\{x\in(0,1/2]\}-1\{x\in(1/2,1)\}.

(S.52)

For each
{\bf i}\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{(0,\ldots,0)\}
, the
tensor
product Haar wavelet

\Psi^{\bf i}:(0,1)^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
is defined by

\Psi^{i}(x):=\psi^{i_{1}}(x_{1})\ldots\psi^{i_{d}}(x_{d}),

(S.53)

where
\psi^{1}(x)=\psi(x)
and
\psi^{0}(x)=1
.
To ease notation, let
\mathcal{I}=\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{(0,\ldots,0)\}
and

\mathcal{K}(\ell)=[2^{\ell}-1]^{d}
. For each
\ell\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\},k\in\mathcal{K}(\ell)
and
{\bf i}\in\mathcal{I}
, put
\Psi_{\ell k}^{{\bf i}}(x):=2^{\ell d/2}\Psi^{{\bf i}}(2^{\ell}x-k)
. Finally, let
\Phi(x)=1\{x\in(0,1)^{d}\}
. The Haar wavelet basis is the collection
\{\Psi_{\ell k}^{{\bf i}}:\ell\in\mathbb{N},k\in\mathcal{K}(\ell),{\bf i}\in\mathcal{I}\}\cup\{\Phi\}
, and it forms an orthonormal basis of
L^{2}((0,1)^{d})
.

We now describe the estimator
\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{wave}}
, which applies hard
thresholding to sample wavelet coefficients. For each
\ell\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\},k\in\mathcal{K}(\ell)
and
{\bf i}\in\mathcal{I}
, write

\theta_{\ell k{\bf i}}(f):=\int_{\Omega}\Psi_{\ell k}^{{\bf i}}(x)f(x)\,dx,\quad\widetilde{\theta}_{\ell k{\bf i}}(f):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}f(x_{j})\Psi_{\ell k}^{{\bf i}}(x_{j}),

for the population-level and empirical wavelet coefficients of a given
f\in L^{2}(\Omega)
. The sample wavelet coefficient is
\widetilde{\theta}_{\ell k{\bf i}}(y_{1:n})
. The hard thresholding estimator we use is defined with respect
to a threshold
\lambda>0
and a truncation level
\ell^{\ast}\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
as

\widehat{\theta}_{\ell k{\bf i}}^{(\lambda,\ell^{\ast})}:=\begin{cases}\widetilde{\theta}_{\ell k{\bf i}}(y_{1:n})\cdot 1\{\widetilde{\theta}_{\ell k{\bf i}}(y_{1:n})\geq\lambda\},&\textrm{$\ell=0,\ldots,\ell^{\ast}$}\\
0,&\textrm{$\ell\geq\log_{2}(n)/d+1,$}\end{cases}

(S.54)

and we map the sequence estimate
\widehat{\theta}^{(\lambda,\ell^{\ast})}=\bigl(\widehat{\theta}_{\ell k{\bf i}}^{(\lambda,\ell^{\ast})}:\ell\in\mathbb{N},k\in\mathcal{K}(\ell),{\bf i}\in\mathcal{I}\bigr)
to the function

\widehat{f}^{(\lambda,\ell^{\ast})}(x)=\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}+\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}(\ell),{\bf i}\in\mathcal{I}}\widehat{\theta}_{\ell k{\bf i}}^{(\lambda,\ell^{\ast})}\Psi_{\ell k}^{{\bf i}}(x),

(S.55)

where
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}y_{i}
is the sample average of the
responses. (
S.55
) defines a family of
estimators depending on the threshold
\lambda
, and the estimator

\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{wave}}
is the hard thresholding estimate

\widehat{f}^{(\lambda,\ell^{\ast})}
with the specific choices
\lambda=8n^{-1/2}\log^{3/2}(2n/\delta)
and
\ell^{\ast}=\log_{2}(n)/d
.

D.7.2
Step 2: Wavelet decay

In this section we recall the wavelet coefficient decay of functions in

\mathrm{BV}(\Omega)
and in
L^{\infty}(\Omega)
. For each
\ell\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
, define

\theta_{\ell\cdot}(f)=\bigl(\theta_{\ell k}^{{{\bf i}}}(f):k\in\mathcal{K}(\ell),{\bf i}\in\mathcal{I}\bigr).

and write
\theta(f)
for the vector with entries
\theta(f)_{\ell}:=\theta_{\ell\cdot}(f)
.

Lemma S.7
.

Let
f\in L^{\infty}(\Omega)
. Then for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
,

\|\theta_{\ell\cdot}(f)\|_{\infty}\leq 2^{-\ell d/2}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}.

(S.56)

Lemma S.8
.

There exists a constant
C_{1}
such that for all
f\in\mathrm{BV}(\Omega)

and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
,

\|\theta_{\ell\cdot}(f)\|_{1}\leq C_{1}2^{-\ell(1-d/2)}\mathrm{TV}(f;\Omega).

(S.57)

The decay rates established by Lemmas
S.7

and
S.8
imply that if
f_{0}\in\mathrm{BV}_{\infty}(L,M)
,
then
\theta(f_{0})
belongs to
\Theta_{\infty}^{0,\infty}(M)
and

\Theta_{\infty}^{1,1}(L)
, where
\Theta_{\infty}^{s,p}(C)
consists of
sequences
\theta
for which

\|\theta\|_{\Theta_{\infty}^{s,p}}:=\sup_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}}2^{\ell(s+d/2-d/p)}\|\theta_{\ell\cdot}\|_{p}<C.

(S.58)

The sets
\Theta_{\infty}^{s,p}(C)
can be interpreted as normed balls in Besov
bodies, since a function
f
belongs to the Besov space
B_{\infty}^{s,p}
if
and only if its coefficients in a suitable wavelet basis satisfy

\|\theta(f)\|_{\Theta_{\infty}^{s,p}}<\infty
.

The conclusions of Lemmas
S.7
and
S.8
are
generally well-understood (see for instance

Giné and Nickl 2021

for the upper bound on wavelet decay of
L^{\infty}(\Omega)
functions when
d=1
, and
Cohen et al. 2003
for the wavelet decay of

\mathrm{BV}(\Omega)
functions). For purposes of completeness only, we include
proofs of these results in
Appendix
G.3.1
.

D.7.3
Step 3: Deterministic upper bound on
\ell^{2}
-error

In this section, we analyze the
\ell^{2}
-error of the hard-thresholding
estimator
\widehat{\theta}^{(\lambda,\ell^{\ast})}
. Specifically, we upper bound
the magnitude of
\|\widehat{\theta}^{(\lambda,\ell^{\ast})}-\theta(f_{0})\|_{2}
as a
function of the
\ell^{\infty}
distance between the (truncated) sample and
population-level wavelet coefficients, i.e the quantity

\epsilon_{n}:=\|(\widetilde{\theta}(y_{1:n})-\theta(f_{0}))_{\leq\ell^{\ast}}\|_{\infty},

where

(\theta_{\leq\ell^{\ast}})_{\ell k}^{{\bf i}}:=\begin{cases}\theta_{\ell k}^{{\bf i}},&\quad{if $\ell\leq\ell^{\ast}$,}\\
0,&\quad{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Note that this upper bound is purely deterministic.

Proposition S.2
.

Suppose
\theta(f_{0})\in\Theta_{\infty}^{0,\infty}(M)\cap\Theta_{\infty}^{1,1}(L)
.
Then there exists a constant
C_{3}
that does not depend on
n,M
or
L
for
which the following statement holds: if
\lambda\geq 2\epsilon_{n}
, then the
estimator
\widehat{\theta}^{(\lambda,\ell^{\ast})}

of (
S.55
) satisfies the upper bound

\|\widehat{\theta}^{(\lambda,\ell^{\ast})}-\theta(f_{0})\|_{2}^{2}\leq 4C_{1}LM2^{-\ell^{\ast}}+C_{3}\cdot\begin{cases}L\lambda\max\{1,1/M,\log_{2}(M/\lambda)\},&\hskip 10.22217pt{if $d=2$}\\
L^{2/d}\lambda^{4/(2+d)}+LM\Bigl(\frac{\lambda}{M}\Bigr)^{2/d},&\hskip 10.22217pt{if $d\geq 3$.}\end{cases}

(S.59)

The proof is deferred to
Appendix
G.3.2
.

D.7.4
Step 4: Uniform convergence of wavelet coefficients

Lemma
S.9
gives an upper bound on the maximum
difference between sample and population-level wavelet coefficients that holds
uniformly over all
\ell=0,\ldots,\log_{2}(n)/d
,
k\in\mathcal{K}(\ell)
and

{\bf i}\in\mathcal{I}
. Its proof is deferred to
Appendix
G.3.3
.

Lemma S.9
.

Suppose we observe data
(x_{1},y_{1}),\ldots,(x_{n},y_{n})
according
to (
1
), where
f_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega;M)
. There exists a
constant
C_{4}
not depending on
n
such that the following statement holds
for all
\delta>0
: with probability at least
1-C_{4}\delta
,

\|(\widetilde{\theta}(y_{1:n})-\theta(f_{0}))_{\leq\log_{2}(n)/d}\|_{\infty}\leq\underbrace{\frac{4\log^{3/2}(2n/\delta)}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\sqrt{12}M\sqrt{\log(2n/\delta)}}{\sqrt{n}}}_{:=\delta_{n}}.

(S.60)

D.7.5
Step 5: Upper bound on risk

We are now ready to prove the stated upper bound (
39
). In
this section we take
\lambda=2\delta_{n}
and
\ell^{\ast}=\log_{2}(n)/d
.
Combining Proposition
S.2
and
Lemma
S.9
, we have that with probability at least
1-C_{4}\delta
,

\|\widehat{\theta}^{(\lambda,\ell^{\ast})}-\theta_{0}(f)\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{4C_{1}LM}{n^{1/d}}+C_{3}\cdot\begin{cases}2L\delta_{n}\max\{1,1/M,\log_{2}(M/2\delta_{n})\},&\quad{if $d=2$,}\\
L^{2/d}(2\delta_{n})^{4/(2+d)}+LM\Bigl(\frac{2\delta_{n}}{M}\Bigr)^{2/d},&\quad{if $d\geq 3$.}\end{cases}

(S.61)

The following lemma allows us to convert this upper bound, which holds with
probability
1-C_{4}\delta
, to an upper bound which holds in expectation.
Its proof is deferred to
Appendix
G.3.4
.

Lemma S.10
.

Let
X>0
be a positive random variable. Suppose there exist positive
numbers
A_{1},\ldots,A_{K}
,
a_{1},\ldots,a_{K}
,
b_{1},\ldots,b_{K}>1
and
B
such
that for all
\delta\in(0,1)
,

\mathbb{P}\Bigl(X>\sum_{k=1}^{K}A_{k}\log^{a_{k}}(b_{k}/\delta)\Bigr)\leq B\delta.

Then there exists a constant
C_{5}
depending only on
a_{1},\ldots,a_{k}
and
B

such that

\mathbb{E}[X]\leq C_{5}\sum_{k=1}^{K}A_{k}(\log b_{k})^{a_{k}}.

Now we use Lemma
S.10
to complete the proof of
Theorem
5
. Note that for any
a>0
,

\delta_{n}^{a}\leq\frac{2^{a}}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigl((\log(2n/\delta))^{3a/2}+M\sqrt{\log(2n/\delta)}\Bigr).

Thus we may can apply Lemma
S.10

to (
S.61
), which, setting
\delta_{n}^{\ast}=\frac{4}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigl((\log 2n)^{3/2}+M\log(n)^{1/2}\Bigr)
, gives

\mathbb{E}\|\widehat{\theta}^{(\lambda,\ell^{\ast})}-\theta_{0}(f)\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{4C_{1}LM}{n^{1/d}}+C_{6}\cdot\begin{cases}2L\delta_{n}^{\ast}\max\{1,1/M,\log_{2}(M\sqrt{n})\},&\quad{if $d=2$,}\\
L^{2/d}(2\delta_{n}^{\ast})^{4/(2+d)}+LM\Bigl(\frac{2\delta_{n}^{\ast}}{M}\Bigr)^{2/d},&\quad{if $d\geq 3$.}\end{cases}

(S.62)

where
C_{6}=2C_{3}C_{5}
.

Finally, we translate this to an upper bound on the expected risk of

\widehat{f}_{\mathrm{wave}}=\widehat{f}^{(\lambda,\ell^{\ast})}
. Since
\{\Psi_{\ell k}^{{\bf i}},\ell\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\},k\in\mathcal{K}(\ell),{\bf i}\in\mathcal{I}\}\cup\{\Phi\}
forms an orthonormal basis of
L^{2}(\Omega)
, by
Parseval’s theorem we have

\|\widehat{f}^{(\lambda,\ell^{\ast})}-f_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}=(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}-\mathbb{E}[f_{0}])^{2}+\|\widehat{\theta}^{(\lambda,\ell^{\ast})}-\theta(f_{0})\|_{2}^{2}.

Taking expectation on both sides, the claimed upper
bound (
39
) follows by (
S.62
),
upon proper choice of constant
C=\max\{4C_{1},16C_{6}\}
.
∎

Appendix E
Analysis of graph TV denoising

In this section, we review tools for analyzing graph total variation denoising.
Suppose an unknown
\theta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and observations

y_{1},\dotsc,y_{n}
,

y_{i}=\theta_{0i}+z_{i},\qquad i=1,\dotsc,n,

(S.63)

where
z_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
. The graph total variation denoising estimator

\hat{\theta}
associated with a graph
G=(V,E)
,
|V|=n
, is given by

\hat{\theta}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{1}{2}\lVert y_{1:n}-\theta\rVert_{2}^{2}+\lambda\lVert D\theta\rVert_{1},

(S.64)

where
D\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times n}
is the edge incidence matrix of
G
.

The initial analysis of graph total variation denoising was performed by

Hutter and Rigollet 2016
for the two-dimensional grid.

Sadhanala et al. 2016
subsequently generalized the analysis to

d
-dimensional lattices, and
Wang et al. 2016
provided tools for the
analysis of general graphs. These techniques rely on direct analysis
of properties of graph
G
and the penalty
D
in induces, which is
tractable when
G
has a known and regular properties (e.g., it is a lattice
graph).

Unfortunately, direct analysis on
D
may not always be feasible. It may
be possible, however, to compare the operator
D
to a
surrogate
operator
whose properties we analyze instead. For our purposes, we compare

D
to a linear operator which first takes averages on a partition, and
then computes differences across cells of the partition. Comparison to this
type of surrogate operator was used by
Padilla et al. 2020
to bound the
risk of graph total variation denoising in probability; the following theorem
provides an analogous risk bound in expectation. We note that elements of the
“surrogate operator analysis” are also found in
Padilla et al. 2018
.

Theorem S.1
.

Suppose we observe data according to model (
S.63
) and
compute the graph TV denoising estimator
\hat{\theta}

of (
S.64
).
Let
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
denote an averaging operator over
\bar{N}
groups of
the form,

A=\begin{bmatrix}n_{1}^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n_{1}}\mathbf{1}_{n_{1}}^{\top}&0&\ldots&0\\
0&n_{2}^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n_{2}}\mathbf{1}_{n_{2}}^{\top}&\ldots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&0&\ldots&n_{\bar{N}}^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n_{\bar{N}}}\mathbf{1}_{n_{\bar{N}}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix},

with
M:=\max_{j}n_{j}
,
and let
\bar{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{\bar{N}\times n}
be the same matrix with redundant
rows removed. Further let
T\in\mathbb{R}^{\bar{m}\times\bar{N}}
be a
surrogate penalty operator, with singular value decomposition

T=U\Sigma V^{\top}
, such that

\displaystyle\lVert T\bar{A}\theta\rVert_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\Phi_{1}(D,T,A)\lVert D\theta\rVert_{1},

(S.65)

\displaystyle\lVert(I-A)\theta\rVert_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\Phi_{2}(D,T,A)\lVert D\theta\rVert_{1},

(S.66)

for quantities

\Phi_{1}(D,T,A),\Phi_{2}(D,T,A)

that may depend on
n
, for all
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. If the penalty parameter

\lambda>\max\left\{8M^{1/2}\Phi_{1}(D,T,A)\cdot\sigma\sqrt{\log(2n^{4})\sum_{k=2}^{\bar{N}}\frac{\lVert u_{k}\rVert_{\infty}^{2}}{\xi_{k}^{2}}},\Phi_{2}(D,T,A)\cdot\sigma\sqrt{2\log(n)}\right\}

(S.67)

where
u_{k}
is the
k
th column of
U
and
\xi_{k}
the
k
th diagonal entry
of
\Sigma
, then there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{n}\lVert\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}\right]\leq C\left(\frac{\lambda\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert_{1}}{n}+\frac{M\mathrm{nullity}(T)}{n}\right).

(S.68)

Proof.

We follow the approach of
Padilla et al. 2020
, with adaptations to
provide a bound in expectation rather than in probability. From the basic
inequality,

\lVert\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq 2\langle{z_{1:n}},\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\rangle+\lambda(\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert_{1}-\lVert D\hat{\theta}\rVert_{1}),

where
{z_{1:n}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
is the vector of error terms
z_{i}
,
i=1,\dotsc,n
.
We provide two deterministic bounds under the “good case” that the error
term falls into the set,

\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}=\left\{{z_{1:n}}:\max\left\{M^{1/2}\Phi_{1}(D,T,A)\sup_{\bar{A}\theta\in\mathrm{row}(T):\lVert T\bar{A}\theta\rVert_{1}\leq 1}|\langle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}},\bar{A}\theta\rangle|,\Phi_{2}(D,T,A)\lVert{z_{1:n}}\rVert_{\infty}\right\}\leq\frac{\lambda}{8}\right\},

(S.69)

where
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\bar{N}}
has entries

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}}_{j}=n_{j}^{1/2}(\bar{A}{z_{1:n}})_{j}
,
and under the “bad case” that
{z_{1:n}}\not\in\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}
.

Upper bound in the “good case”.

Decompose the first term on the RHS,

\displaystyle\langle{z_{1:n}},\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{z_{1:n}},\hat{\theta}-A\hat{\theta}\rangle+\langle{z_{1:n}},A\theta_{0}-\theta_{0}\rangle+\langle{z_{1:n}},A(\theta_{0}-\hat{\theta})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\langle{z_{1:n}},A(\theta_{0}-\hat{\theta})\rangle+\lVert{z_{1:n}}\rVert_{\infty}(\lVert(I-A)\hat{\theta}\rVert_{1}+\lVert(I-A)\theta_{0}\rVert_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\langle{z_{1:n}},A(\theta_{0}-\hat{\theta})\rangle+\lVert{z_{1:n}}\rVert_{\infty}\Phi(D,T,A)(\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert_{1}+\lVert D\hat{\theta}\rVert_{1}),

where the final inequality follows from (
S.66
).
Observe that we may rewrite, for any
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\langle{z_{1:n}},A\theta\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{\bar{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{j}}{z_{1:n}}_{(\sum_{k=1}^{j-1}n_{k})+i}(\bar{A}\theta)_{j}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\sum_{j=1}^{\bar{N}}n_{j}^{1/2}{z_{1:n}}_{j}(\bar{A}\theta)_{j}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\langle{z_{1:n}},A\theta\rangle

\displaystyle\leq M^{1/2}|\langle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}},\bar{A}\theta\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq M^{1/2}\left|\langle\proj_{V}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}}),\bar{A}\theta\rangle+\langle\proj_{V^{\perp}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}}),\bar{A}\theta\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq M^{1/2}\left|\lVert\proj_{V}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}})\rVert_{2}\lVert\bar{A}\theta\rVert_{2}+\langle\proj_{V^{\perp}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}}),\bar{A}\theta\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq M^{1/2}\left(\lVert\proj_{V}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}})\rVert_{2}\lVert\theta\rVert_{2}+|\langle\proj_{V^{\perp}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}}),\bar{A}\theta\rangle|\right|,

where
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\bar{N}}
has independent
\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})

entries and
V=\mathrm{null}(T)
.
Substitute back in to obtain,

\displaystyle\lVert\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2M^{1/2}(\lVert\proj_{V}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}})\rVert_{2}\lVert\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\rVert_{2}+|\langle\proj_{V^{\perp}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}}),\bar{A}(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0})\rangle|)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+2\lVert{z_{1:n}}\rVert_{\infty}\Phi(D,T,A)(\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert_{1}+\lVert D\hat{\theta}\rVert_{1})+\lambda(\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert_{1}-\lVert D\hat{\theta}\rVert_{1}),

and consequently,

\displaystyle\lVert\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\rVert_{2}(

\displaystyle\lVert\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\rVert_{2}-2M^{1/2}\lVert\proj_{V}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}})\rVert_{2})

\displaystyle\leq 2M^{1/2}|\langle\proj_{V^{\perp}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}}),\bar{A}(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0})\rangle|+2\lVert{z_{1:n}}\rVert_{\infty}\Phi(D,T,A)(\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert_{1}+\lVert D\hat{\theta}\rVert_{1})+\lambda(\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert_{1}-\lVert D\hat{\theta}\rVert_{1})

Case 1.

\lVert\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\rVert_{2}\leq 4M^{1/2}\lVert\proj_{V}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}})\rVert_{2}
.

Case 2.

\lVert\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\rVert_{2}>4M^{1/2}\lVert\proj_{V}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}})\rVert_{2}
. Then,

\displaystyle\lVert\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4M^{1/2}|\langle\proj_{V^{\perp}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}}),\bar{A}(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0})\rangle|+4\lVert{z_{1:n}}\rVert_{\infty}\Phi(D,T,A)(\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert_{1}+\lVert D\hat{\theta}\rVert_{1})+\lambda(\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert_{1}-\lVert D\hat{\theta}\rVert_{1}).

We then bound,

\displaystyle|\langle\proj_{V^{\perp}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}}),\bar{A}(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0})\rangle|

\displaystyle=\left|\left\langle\proj_{V^{\perp}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}}),\frac{\bar{A}(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0})}{\lVert T\bar{A}(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0})\rVert_{1}}\right\rangle\lVert T\bar{A}(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0})\rVert_{1}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\bar{A}\theta\in V^{\perp}:\lVert T\bar{A}\theta\rVert_{1}\leq 1}|\langle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}},\bar{A}\theta\rangle|\lVert T\bar{A}(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0})\rVert_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\bar{A}\theta\in V^{\perp}:\lVert T\bar{A}\theta\rVert_{1}\leq 1}|\langle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}},\bar{A}\theta\rangle|\Phi(D,T,A)(\lVert D\hat{\theta}\rVert_{1}+\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert_{1}),

where the last inequality follows by (
S.66
).
Conditioning on
{z_{1:n}}\in\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}
, we find that under Case 2,

\displaystyle\lVert\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda}{2}(\lVert D\hat{\theta}\rVert_{1}+\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert_{1})+\frac{\lambda}{2}(\lVert D\hat{\theta}\rVert_{1}+\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert_{1})+\lambda(\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert_{1}-\lVert D\hat{\theta}\rVert_{1})

\displaystyle\leq 2\lambda\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert_{1}.

Therefore, conditioning on
{z_{1:n}}\in\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}
and combining Case 1
and Case 2, we obtain that

\displaystyle\lVert\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 16M\lVert\proj_{V}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}})\rVert_{2}^{2}+2\lambda\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert_{1}.

Upper bound in the “bad case”.

On the “bad event” that
{z_{1:n}}\not\in\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}
, we apply
Hölder directly to the basic inequality to bound,

\displaystyle\lVert\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq 2\lVert{z_{1:n}}\rVert_{2}\lVert\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\rVert_{2}+\lambda\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert_{1},

and rearrange to obtain,

\lVert\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq 16\lVert{z_{1:n}}\rVert_{2}^{2}+2\lambda\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert_{1}.

(S.70)

Combining the “good case” and “bad case” upper bounds.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\lVert\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\lVert\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}\mathbf{1}\{{z_{1:n}}\in\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}\}+\lVert\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}\mathbf{1}\{{z_{1:n}}\not\in\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[16M\lVert\proj_{V}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}})\rVert_{2}^{2}+2\lambda\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert_{1}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[(16\lVert{z_{1:n}}\rVert_{2}^{2}+2\lambda\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert_{1})\mathbf{1}\{{z_{1:n}}\not\in\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}\}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\left[16M\mathrm{dim}(V)+4\lambda\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert_{1}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\lVert{z_{1:n}}\rVert_{2}^{4}]}\cdot\mathbb{P}[{z_{1:n}}\not\in\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}]\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\left[16M\mathrm{dim}(V)+4\lambda\lVert D\theta_{0}\rVert_{1}+\sqrt{3}n\cdot\mathbb{P}[{z_{1:n}}\not\in\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}]\right]

It remains to bound the probability of the bad case,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{{z_{1:n}}\not\in\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{M^{1/2}\sup_{\bar{A}\theta\in V^{\perp}:\lVert T\bar{A}\theta\rVert_{1}\leq 1}|\langle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}},\bar{A}\theta\rangle|\geq\lambda/8\Phi(D,T,A)\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\lVert{z_{1:n}}\rVert_{\infty}\geq\lambda/8\Phi(D,T,A)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\{M^{1/2}\Phi(D,T,A)\lVert(T^{+})^{\top}\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}}\rVert_{\infty}\geq\lambda/8\}+\mathbb{P}\{\Phi(D,T,A)\lVert{z_{1:n}}\rVert_{\infty}\geq\lambda/8\}.

Standard results on the maxima of Gaussians provide that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{M^{1/2}\Phi_{1}(D,T,A)\lVert(T^{+})^{\top}\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{{z_{1:n}}}\rVert_{\infty}\geq M^{1/2}\Phi_{1}(D,T,A)\cdot\sigma\sqrt{\log(2n^{2}/\delta)\cdot\sum_{k=2}^{\bar{N}}\frac{\lVert u_{k}\rVert_{\infty}^{2}}{\xi_{k}^{2}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\delta,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\Phi_{2}(D,T,A)\lVert{z_{1:n}}\rVert_{\infty}\geq\Phi_{2}(D,T,A)\cdot\sigma\sqrt{\log(2n^{2}/\delta)}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\delta.

Recalling the choice of penalty parameter,

\lambda>\max\left\{8M^{1/2}\Phi_{1}(D,T,A)\cdot\sigma\sqrt{\log(2n^{4})\sum_{k=2}^{\bar{N}}\frac{\lVert u_{k}\rVert_{\infty}^{2}}{\xi_{k}^{2}}},\Phi_{2}(D,T,A)\cdot\sigma\sqrt{2\log(n)}\right\},

we conclude that

\mathbb{P}\{{z_{1:n}}\not\in\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}\}\leq\frac{2}{n^{2}},

completing the proof.
∎

We now state a well-known result controlling certain functionals of the lattice
difference operator. These quantities have been analyzed by others studying
graph total variation denoising on lattices, e.g.,

Hutter and Rigollet 2016
and
Sadhanala et al. 2017
.

Lemma S.11
.

Let
T
be the edge incidence operator of the
d
-dimensional
lattice graph
N
elements per direction. Denote
n=N^{d}
.
The left singular vectors of
T
satisfy an incoherence condition,

\lVert u_{j}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\frac{C_{d}}{\sqrt{n}},\qquad j=1,\dotsc,n,

for some
C_{d}>0
, and its singular values satisfy an
asymptotic scaling,

c_{d}(j/n)^{1/d}\leq\xi_{j}\leq C_{d}(j/n)^{1/d},\qquad j=2,\dotsc,n,

for some
0<c_{d}<C_{d}
. Consequently,

\sum_{j=2}^{n}\frac{\lVert u_{j}\rVert_{\infty}^{2}}{\xi_{j}^{2}}=C_{d}\begin{cases}\log n&d=2,\\
1&d>2.\end{cases}

(S.71)

Appendix F
Embeddings for random graphs

We begin by providing a result that controls the number of sample points
that fall into each cell of a lattice mesh.

Lemma S.12
.

Suppose
x_{1},\dotsc,x_{n}
are sampled from a distribution
P

supported on
(0,1)^{d}
with density
p
such that
0<p_{\mathrm{min}}<p(x)<p_{\mathrm{max}}<1
for
all
x\in(0,1)^{d}
. Form a partition of
(0,1)^{d}
using an equally spaced
mesh with
N=C_{1}(p_{\mathrm{min}}n/\log^{\alpha}n)^{1/d}
,
\alpha>1
, along each
dimension. Let
\mathcal{C}_{\ell}
denote the
\ell
th cell of the mesh, and
let
|\mathcal{C}_{\ell}|
denote its empirical content.
Then for all
x_{1:n}\in\mathscr{X}_{1}
, with

\mathbb{P}\{x_{1:n}\in\mathscr{X}_{1}\}\geq 1-2/n^{4}
,

\displaystyle\max_{\ell}|\mathcal{C}_{\ell}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\log^{\alpha}n,

(S.72)

\displaystyle\min_{\ell}|\mathcal{C}_{\ell}|

\displaystyle\geq c_{4}\log^{\alpha}n,

(S.73)

for
n
sufficiently large, where
C_{3},c_{4}>0
depend only on

p_{\mathrm{min}},p_{\mathrm{max}},d
.

Proof.

From standard concentration bounds
(e.g.,
Von Luxburg et al. 2014
; Proposition 27)
on a random variable
m\sim\mathrm{Bin}(n,p)
, for all
\delta\in(0,1]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{m\geq(1+\delta)np\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\{-\frac{1}{3}\delta^{2}np\},

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{m\leq(1-\delta)np\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\{-\frac{1}{3}\delta^{2}np\}.

Apply these bounds with
p=\mathbb{P}\{x\in\mathcal{C}_{\ell}\}
to obtain that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\ell}|\mathcal{C}_{\ell}|\geq(1+\delta)C_{1}^{d}\frac{p_{\mathrm{max}}}{p_{\mathrm{min}}}\log^{\alpha}n\right\}

\displaystyle\leq N^{d}\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{3}\delta^{2}C_{1}^{d}\log^{\alpha}n\right\},

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\min_{\ell}|\mathcal{C}_{\ell}|\leq(1-\delta)C_{1}^{d}\log^{\alpha}n\right\}

\displaystyle\leq N^{d}\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{3}\delta^{2}C_{1}^{d}\log^{\alpha}n\right\},

for all
\delta\in(0,1)
. Setting the RHS to
1/n^{4}
,

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}^{d}p_{\mathrm{min}}n}{\log^{\alpha}n}\exp\{-\frac{1}{3}\delta^{2}C_{1}^{-d}\log^{\alpha}n\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{4}}

\displaystyle\log(C_{1}^{d}p_{\mathrm{min}})-\log(\log^{\alpha}n)-\frac{1}{3}\delta^{2}C_{1}^{-d}\log^{\alpha}n

\displaystyle\leq-5\log n

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\frac{1}{3}\delta^{2}C_{1}^{-d}\log^{\alpha}n

\displaystyle\geq 5\log n+\log(C_{1}^{d}p_{\mathrm{min}})-\log(\log^{\alpha}n)

\displaystyle\delta^{2}

\displaystyle\geq 3C_{1}^{d}\left(5\log^{1-\alpha}n+\frac{\log(C_{1}^{d}p_{\mathrm{min}})}{\log^{\alpha}n}-\frac{\log(\log^{\alpha}n)}{\log^{\alpha}n}\right)

\displaystyle\delta

\displaystyle\geq C_{2}\log^{(1-\alpha)/2}n,

for some
C_{2}>0
for all
n
sufficiently large. Therefore deduce that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\ell}|\mathcal{C}_{\ell}|\geq C_{1}^{d}\frac{p_{\mathrm{max}}}{p_{\mathrm{min}}}\log^{\alpha}n+C_{1}^{d}C_{2}\frac{p_{\mathrm{max}}}{p_{\mathrm{min}}}\log^{(1+\alpha)/2}n\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{4}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\min_{\ell}|\mathcal{C}_{\ell}|\leq C_{1}^{d}\log^{\alpha}n-C_{1}^{d}C_{2}\log^{(1+\alpha)/2}n\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{4}}.

Recall that
\alpha>1
by assumption, and choose
C_{3},c_{4}>0
with
n

sufficiently large to obtain the claim.
∎

The following lemma establishes embeddings from certain random graphs into
a coarser lattice graph.

Lemma S.13
.

Partition the domain
(0,1)^{d}
using an equally spaced mesh with

N=C_{1}(p_{\mathrm{min}}n/\log^{\alpha}n)^{1/d}
elements per direction. Suppose
that
x_{1:n}\in\mathscr{X}_{1}
, with
x_{1:n}
re-indexed such that

\displaystyle x_{1},\dotsc,x_{|\mathcal{C}_{1}|}

\displaystyle\in\mathcal{C}_{1},

\displaystyle x_{|\mathcal{C}_{1}|+1},\dotsc,x_{|\mathcal{C}_{1}|+|\mathcal{C}_{2}|}

\displaystyle\in\mathcal{C}_{2},

\displaystyle x_{\sum_{\ell=1}^{N^{d}-1}|\mathcal{C}_{\ell}|+1},\dotsc,x_{N^{d}}

\displaystyle\in\mathcal{C}_{N^{d}}.

Consider the averaging operators,

A=\begin{bmatrix}|\mathcal{C}_{1}|^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{|\mathcal{C}_{1}|}\mathbf{1}_{|\mathcal{C}_{1}|}^{\top}&0&\ldots&0\\
0&|\mathcal{C}_{2}|^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{|\mathcal{C}_{2}|}\mathbf{1}_{|\mathcal{C}_{2}|}^{\top}&\ldots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&0&\ldots&|\mathcal{C}_{N^{d}}|^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{|\mathcal{C}_{N^{d}}|}\mathbf{1}_{|\mathcal{C}_{N^{d}}|}^{\top}\end{bmatrix},

(S.74)

and the lattice difference operator
T
based on the graph

G_{T}=(\{1,\dotsc,N^{d}\},E_{T}),

(S.75)

where
(i,j)\in E_{T}
if the midpoints of
\mathcal{C}_{i},\mathcal{C}_{j}
are

1/N
apart. Also, let
\bar{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{d}\times n}
be the matrix obtained
by dropping the redundant rows of
A
.

•

Build the Voronoi graph from
x_{1:n}
, and let
{\tilde{D}^{{\mathrm{V}}}}
denote
the edge incidence operator with edge set
E^{\mathrm{V}}

and edge weights

\tilde{w}^{\mathrm{V}}_{ij}=\max\{c_{0}n^{-(d-1)/d},w^{\mathrm{V}}_{ij}\}
for each

i,j
. Further
condition on the set
\mathscr{X}_{2}
such that the result of
Lemma
S.14
holds with probability
1-1/n^{4}

(equivalently, the set that the result
of Lemma
S.15
holds with
probability
1-1/n^{4}
). Then there
exists a constant
C_{6}>0
such that for all
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\lVert T\bar{A}\theta\rVert_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{6}n^{(d-1)/d}\lVert{\tilde{D}^{{\mathrm{V}}}}\theta\rVert_{1}.

(S.76)

\displaystyle\lVert(I-A)\theta\rVert_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{6}(\log n)^{\alpha}n^{(d-1)/d}\lVert{\tilde{D}^{{\mathrm{V}}}}\theta\rVert_{1},

(S.77)

•

Build the Voronoi graph from
x_{1:n}
, and let
{\check{D}^{{\mathrm{V}}}}
denote
the edge incidence operator with edge set
E^{\mathrm{V}}

and edge weights

\check{w}^{\mathrm{V}}_{ij}=1
for each
i,j
such that
w^{\mathrm{V}}_{i,j}>0
.
Further condition on the set
\mathscr{X}_{2}
such that the result of
Lemma
S.14
holds with probability
1-1/n^{4}
. Then there
exists a constant
C_{7}>0
such that for all
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\lVert T\bar{A}\theta\rVert_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{7}\lVert{\check{D}^{{\mathrm{V}}}}\theta\rVert_{1}.

(S.78)

\displaystyle\lVert(I-A)\theta\rVert_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{7}(\log n)^{\alpha}\lVert{\check{D}^{{\mathrm{V}}}}\theta\rVert_{1},

(S.79)

•

Build the
\varepsilon
-neighborhood graph from
x_{1:n}
, with

\varepsilon\geq 2\sqrt{d}/N
. Then with the constant
c_{4}
from
Lemma
S.12
, it holds that for all

\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\lVert T\bar{A}\theta\rVert_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{c_{4}^{2}\log^{2\alpha}n}\lVert{D^{\varepsilon}}\theta\rVert_{1}.

(S.80)

\displaystyle\lVert(I-A)\theta\rVert_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{c_{4}\log^{\alpha}n}\lVert{D^{\varepsilon}}\theta\rVert_{1},

(S.81)

•

Build the
k
-nearest neighbors graph from
x_{1:n}
, with

k\geq C_{5}\log^{3}n
. Further condition on the
set
\mathscr{X}_{2}
such that the result of
Lemma
S.14
holds with probability
1-1/n^{4}
.
Then with the constant
c_{4}
from Lemma
S.12
, it
holds for all
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\lVert T\bar{A}\theta\rVert_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{c_{4}^{2}\log^{2\alpha}n}\lVert{D^{k}}\theta\rVert_{1}.

(S.82)

\displaystyle\lVert(I-A)\theta\rVert_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{c_{4}\log^{\alpha}n}\lVert{D^{k}}\theta\rVert_{1},

(S.83)

Proof.

\varepsilon
-neighborhood graph.

First, we prove (
S.80
) and
(
S.81
). For the former, observe that

\displaystyle\lVert T\bar{A}\theta\rVert_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{(k,\ell)\in E_{T}}\left||\mathcal{C}_{k}|^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{C}_{k}}\theta_{i}-|\mathcal{C}_{\ell}|^{-1}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{C}_{\ell}}\theta_{j}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{(k,\ell)\in E_{T}}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{C}_{k}||\mathcal{C}_{\ell}|}\sum_{i\in|\mathcal{C}_{k}|,j\in|\mathcal{C}_{\ell}|}|\theta_{i}-\theta_{j}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{c_{4}^{2}\log^{2\alpha}n}\sum_{(k,\ell)\in E_{T}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{C}_{k},j\in\mathcal{C}_{\ell}}|\theta_{i}-\theta_{j}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{c_{4}^{2}\log^{2\alpha}n}\lVert{D^{\varepsilon}}\theta\rVert_{1},

as
\varepsilon=2\sqrt{d}/N
. For the latter, similarly deduce that

\displaystyle\lVert(I-A)\theta\rVert_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\theta_{i}-|\mathcal{C}(i)|^{-1}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{C}(i)}\theta_{j}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\mathcal{C}(i)|^{-1}\left|\sum_{j\in\mathcal{C}(i)}\theta_{j}-\theta_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\mathcal{C}(i)|^{-1}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{C}(i)}|\theta_{i}-\theta_{j}|

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{N^{d}}|\mathcal{C}_{\ell}|^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{C}_{\ell}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{C}_{\ell}}|\theta_{i}-\theta_{j}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{c_{4}\log^{\alpha}n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{N^{d}}\sum_{i<j\in\mathcal{C}_{\ell}}|\theta_{i}-\theta_{j}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{c_{4}\log^{\alpha}n}\lVert{D^{\varepsilon}}\theta\rVert_{1}.

k
-nearest neighbors graph.

Recall that we have conditioned on the set
\mathscr{X}_{2}
such that the result
of Lemma
S.14
holds. In particular,
(
S.86
) gives that

\min_{i=1,\dotsc,n}\varepsilon_{k}(x_{i})\geq C\left(\frac{k}{n}\right)^{1/d},

where
\varepsilon_{k}(x_{i}):=\lVert x_{i}-x_{(k)}(x_{i})\rVert_{2}
.
The results (
S.82
) and
(
S.83
) then follow by observing that on the
event
\mathscr{X}_{2}
, the
k
-nearest neighbors graph with
k\geq C_{5}\log^{3}n

dominates the
\varepsilon
-neighborhood graph with
\varepsilon=2\sqrt{d}/N
.

Voronoi adjacency graph.

We will prove the results
(
S.78
) and
(
S.79
) by providing a graph comparison inequality
between the
\varepsilon
-neighborhood graph with

\varepsilon=2\sqrt{d}/N
and the Voronoi adjacency graph.
The results
(
S.76
), (
S.77
)
follow from the inequality

\lVert{\check{D}^{{\mathrm{V}}}}\theta\rVert_{1}\leq c_{0}^{-1}n^{(d-1)/d}\lVert{\tilde{D}^{{\mathrm{V}}}}\theta\rVert_{1}
for all
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Intuition and outline.
The central goal of this proof is to show that

\lVert{D^{\varepsilon}}\theta\rVert_{1}\leq C(n)\lVert{\check{D}^{{\mathrm{V}}}}\theta\rVert_{1},

for all
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, where
C(n)
is at most polylogarithmic in
n
.
This will be accomplished by

(i)

verifying that for any
\{x_{i},x_{j}\}\in E_{\varepsilon}
, there exists a
path
\{x_{i},x_{k_{1}}\},\{x_{k_{1}},x_{k_{2}}\},\dotsc,\{x_{k_{ij}},x_{j}\}\in E_{n,\mathrm{V}}
, and

(ii)

showing that if one uses the shortest path in the Voronoi
adjacency graph
G_{n,\mathrm{V}}
to connect each
\{x_{i},x_{j}\}\in E_{\varepsilon}
,
then no one edge is used more than
C_{9}\log^{2\alpha}n
times,
where
C_{9}
is a positive constant and
\alpha>1
may be chosen.

Step (i).
Consider
x_{i},x_{j}
such that
\{x_{i},x_{j}\}\in E_{\varepsilon}
.
We will show the existence of a path between
x_{i}
and
x_{j}
in
G_{n,\mathrm{V}}

and also characterize some properties of the path for step (ii).

By definition,
\lVert x_{i}-x_{j}\rVert\leq\varepsilon
.
Denote

\displaystyle x_{ij}

\displaystyle:=\frac{x_{i}+x_{j}}{2},

\displaystyle r_{ij}

\displaystyle:=\lVert x_{i}-x_{ij}\rVert.

Consider the subgraph
G^{ij}=(V^{ij},E^{ij})
, where

\displaystyle V^{ij}

\displaystyle:=\{V_{k}:V_{k}\cap B(x_{ij},r_{ij})\neq\emptyset\},

\displaystyle E^{ij}

\displaystyle:=\{\{V_{k},V_{\ell}\}:V_{k},V_{\ell}\in V^{ij},\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial V_{k}\cap\partial V_{\ell})>0\},

where
B(x_{ij},r_{ij})
is the closed ball centered at
x_{ij}
with
radius
r_{ij}
. By construction,
x_{i},x_{j}\in V^{ij}
, and
by Lemma
S.16
,

G^{ij}
is connected. Therefore a path between
x_{i}
and
x_{j}

exists in the graph
G^{ij}
(one can use, e.g., breadth-first search or
Dijkstra’s algorithm to find such a path).

Step (ii).

For any
\{x_{i},x_{j}\}\in E_{\varepsilon}\setminus E_{n,\mathrm{V}}
, we create a path in

G_{n,\mathrm{V}}
as prescribed in step (i). With these paths created, we upper
bound the number of times any edge in
E_{n,\mathrm{V}}
is used. We do so by
uniformly bounding above the number of times a vertex
x_{k}
appears in
these paths (and since each edge involves two vertices, this immediately
yields an upper bound on the number of times an edge appears in these
paths). We split this into two substeps:

(a)

first, we derive a necessary condition for
x_{k}
to appear in the path
between
x_{i}
and
x_{j}
;

(b)

then, we will upper bound the number of possible pairs
x_{i},x_{j}

such that this necessary condition is satisfied.

Step (ii a).

For
x_{k}
to appear in the path between
x_{i}
and
x_{j}
as designed in
step (i), it is necessary for
V_{k}\in V^{ij}
. Consider

x\in V_{k}\cap B(x_{ij},r_{ij})
. Since
x
belongs to the Voronoi
cell
V_{k}
,

\lVert x-x_{k}\rVert<\min\{\lVert x-x_{i}\rVert,\lVert x-x_{j}\rVert\},

but since
x
also lies in
B(x_{ij},r_{ij})
,

\lVert x-x_{ij}\rVert<r_{ij}.

It follows that,

\displaystyle\lVert x_{k}-x_{ij}\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\lVert x-x_{k}\rVert+\lVert x-x_{ij}\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\lVert x-x_{i}\rVert+\lVert x-x_{ij}\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\lVert x-x_{ij}\rVert+\lVert x_{i}-x_{ij}\rVert+\lVert x-x_{ij}\rVert

\displaystyle\leq 3r_{ij},

thus if
V_{k}\in V^{ij}
, then it is necessary for

x_{k}\in B(x_{ij},3r_{ij})
.

Step (ii b).

Recalling
\varepsilon=2\sqrt{d}/N
, where

N=C_{1}(p_{\mathrm{min}}n/\log^{\alpha}n)^{1/d}
, we have a uniform upper bound of

\max_{\{x_{i},x_{j}\}\in E_{\varepsilon}}r_{ij}\leq C_{8}\left(\frac{\log^{\alpha}n}{n}\right)^{1/d},

for some
C_{8}>0
. Thus, we conclude that for an edge of

x_{k}
to be involved in a path between
x_{i}
and
x_{j}
, it is necessary
for

x_{ij}\in B(x_{k},3C_{8}(\log n/n)^{1/d}),

or more loosely,

x_{i},x_{j}\in B(x_{k},4C_{8}(\log n/n)^{1/d})

recalling that
r_{ij}=\lVert x_{ij}-x_{i}\rVert=\lVert x_{ij}-x_{j}\rVert

and the uniform upper bound on
r_{ij}
. Therefore, the number of paths
in which any
x_{k}
may appear is bounded above,

\displaystyle(nP_{n}(\cdot,4C_{8}(\log^{\alpha}n/n)^{1/d}))^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{9}\log^{2\alpha}n,

where the final inequality is obtained
by (
S.85
).
∎

Appendix G
Auxiliary lemmas and proofs

G.1
Useful concentration results

The following is an immediate consequence of the well-known fact that the set
of balls
B
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
has VC dimension
d+1
, e.g., Lemma 16
of
(
Chaudhuri and Dasgupta 2010
)
.

Lemma S.14
.

Suppose
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}
are drawn from
P
satisfying
Assumption
Assumption A1
. There exist
constants
C_{1}
-
C_{5}
depending only on
d
,
p_{\mathrm{min}}
, and
p_{\mathrm{max}}
such that
the following statements hold: with
probability at least
1-\delta
, for any
z\in\Omega
,

\left\{|B(z,r)\cap\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\}|=0\right\}\Longrightarrow\left\{r<C_{1}\biggl(\frac{\log n+\log(1/\delta)}{n}\biggr)^{1/d}\right\},

(S.84)

and

\displaystyle\left\{r<C_{2}\left(\frac{k-C_{3}(d\log n+\log(1/\delta)+\sqrt{k(d\log n+\log(1/\delta))})}{n}\right)^{1/d}\right\}

(S.85)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\left\{|B(z,r)\cap\{x_{1},\dotsc,x_{n}\}|<k\right\}.

In particular, if
k\geq C_{4}(\log(1/\delta))^{2}\log n
, then

\left\{|B(z,r)\cap\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\}|\geq k\right\}\Longrightarrow\left\{r\geq C_{5}\biggl(\frac{k}{n}\biggr)^{1/d}\right\}.

(S.86)

G.2
Properties of the Voronoi diagram

G.2.1
High probability control of cell geometry

The following lemma shows that with high probability, no Voronoi cell is very
large. Let
r(V_{i}):=\max\{\|x-x_{i}\|:x\in V_{i}\}
be the radius of the
Voronoi cell
V_{i}
.

Lemma S.15
.

Suppose
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}
are drawn from
P
satisfying
Assumption
Assumption A1
.
There exist constants
C_{1}
and

C_{2}
such that the following statement holds: for any
\delta\in(0,1)
,
with probability at least
1-\delta

\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}r(V_{i})\leq C_{1}\biggl(\frac{\log n+\log(1/\delta)}{n}\biggr)^{1/d},

(S.87)

and

\max_{i=1,\ldots,n}\mu(V_{i})\leq C_{2}\biggl(\frac{\log n+\log(1/\delta)}{n}\biggr).

(S.88)

Proof.

If
x\in V_{i}
, then
|B(x,\frac{1}{2}\|x-x_{i}\|)\cap\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\}|=0
. (Note that the same holds true if
\frac{1}{2}
is replaced with any
a\in[0,1)
). Taking
x
to be such that
\|x-x_{i}\|=r(V_{i})
, it follows by
Lemma
S.14
that

\frac{1}{2}r(V_{i})=\frac{1}{2}\|x-x_{i}\|\leq C\biggl(\frac{\log n+\log(1/\delta)}{n}\biggr)^{1/d},

with probability at least
1-\delta
. Multiplying both sides by
2
and
taking a maximum over
i=1,\ldots,n
gives (
S.87
). The
upper bound (
S.88
) on the maximum Lebesgue measure of
V_{i}

follows immediately, since
V_{i}\subseteq B(x,r(V_{i}))
.
∎

G.2.2
Connectedness of the Voronoi adjacency graph

The following lemma relates graph theoretic connectedness to a kind of
topological connectedness that excludes connectedness using sets of

\mathcal{H}^{d-1}
-measure zero.

Lemma S.16
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be open such that there does
not
exist
any set
S\subsetneq\Omega
with
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(S)=0
such that

\Omega\setminus S
is disconnected. Let
\{V_{1},\dotsc,V_{m}\}

denote an open polyhedral partition of
\Omega
. Then the graph

G=(\{V_{1},\dotsc,V_{m}\},E)
, where

E=\{\{V_{i},V_{j}\}:\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial V_{i}\cap\partial V_{j})>0\},

is connected.

Proof.

Assume by way of contradiction that
G
is disconnected. Therefore there
exists sets of vertices
\mathcal{C}_{1}
,
\mathcal{C}_{2}
such that

\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\bar{V}_{i}\cap\bar{V}_{j})=0,

(S.89)

for all
V_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{1}
,
V_{j}\in\mathcal{C}_{2}
. Next, define

\displaystyle\Omega_{1}

\displaystyle:=(\cup_{V_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{1}}\bar{V}_{i})^{\circ},

\displaystyle\Omega_{2}

\displaystyle:=(\cup_{V_{j}\in\mathcal{C}_{2}}\bar{V}_{j})^{\circ},

such that
\{\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2}\}
constitutes an open partition of
\Omega
.
Let

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle:=\Omega\setminus(\Omega_{1}\cup\Omega_{2})

(S.90)

\displaystyle=\Omega\cap((\partial\Omega_{1}\cap\partial\Omega_{2})\cup((\Omega_{1}^{c})^{\circ}\cap(\Omega_{2}^{c})^{\circ}))

\displaystyle=\Omega\cap((\partial\Omega_{1}\cap\partial\Omega_{2})\cup(\Omega_{2}\cap\Omega_{1}))

\displaystyle=\Omega\cap\partial\Omega_{1}\cap\partial\Omega_{2}.

(S.91)

From (
S.89
)
and (
S.91
) we see that

\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(S)=0
. On the other hand,
(
S.90
) yields that

\Omega\setminus S=\Omega_{1}\cup\Omega_{2}
is disconnected
(
\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2}
are open and disjoint).
∎

G.2.3
Analysis of the Voronoi kernel

Recall that in the proof of Theorem
1
, we
compare Voronoi TV to a U-statistic involving the kernel function

H_{\mathrm{Vor}}(x,y)=\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(\partial V_{x_{1}}\cap\partial V_{x_{2}})|x_{1}=x,x_{2}=y]=\int_{L\cap\Omega}(1-p_{x}(z))^{(n-2)}\,dz.

The following lemma shows that this kernel function is close to a spherically
symmetric kernel.

Lemma S.17
.

Suppose
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}
are sampled from distribution
P

satisfying
Assumption A1
. There exist constants
C_{1}
-
C_{4}>0
such that for
h=h_{n}=C_{1}(3\log n/n)^{1/d}
, the following statements
hold.

•

For any
x,y\in\Omega_{h}
,

H_{\mathrm{Vor}}(x,y)=\frac{\eta_{d-2}}{(np(x))^{\frac{d-1}{d}}}K_{\mathrm{Vor}}\biggl(\frac{\|y-x\|}{\varepsilon_{(1)}}\biggr)+O\biggl(\frac{1}{n^{3}}+\frac{(\log n)^{2}}{n}1\bigl\{\|x-y\|\leq C_{2}(\log n/n)^{1/d}\bigr\}\biggr)

(S.92)

•

For any
x,y\in\Omega
,

H_{\mathrm{Vor}}(x,y)\leq\frac{C_{3}}{n^{(d-1)/d}}K_{\mathrm{Vor}}\biggl(\frac{\|y-x\|}{C_{4}n^{1/d}}\biggr).

(S.93)

Proof of (
S.92
).

We now replace the integral above with one involving an exponential function
that can be more easily evaluated. Then we evaluate this latter integral.

Step 1: Reduction to easier integral.

Let
\Omega_{x}=\{z\in\Omega:\mathrm{dist}(z,\partial\Omega)>\|z-x\|\}
. (Note that
L\cap\Omega_{x}=L\cap\Omega_{y}
.) Separate the
integral into two parts,

\displaystyle\int_{L\cap\Omega}\Bigl(1-p_{x}(z)\Bigr)^{(n-2)}\,dz

\displaystyle=\int_{L\cap\Omega_{x}}\Bigl(1-p_{x}(z)\Bigr)^{(n-2)}\,dz+\int_{L\cap(\Omega\setminus\Omega_{x})}\Bigl(1-p_{x}(z)\Bigr)^{(n-2)}\,dz.

We start by showing that the second term above is negligible for
x,y\in\Omega_{h}
. For any
z\in\Omega\setminus\Omega_{x}
, it follows by the
triangle inequality that

\mathrm{dist}(x,\partial\Omega)\leq\|x-z\|+\mathrm{dist}(z,\Omega)\leq 2\|x-z\|.

Since
x\in\Omega_{h}
, it follows that
p_{x}(z)\geq(p_{\min}/2d)\|z-x\|^{d}\geq(p_{\min}/2^{d+1}d)(\mathrm{dist}(x,\partial\Omega))^{d}\geq(p_{\min}/2^{d+1}d)h^{d}
. Integrating over
z\in\Omega\setminus\Omega_{x}
implies an upper bound on the second term,

\displaystyle\int_{L\cap(\Omega\setminus\Omega_{x})}\Bigl(1-p_{x}(z)\Bigr)^{(n-2)}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq\int_{L\cap(\Omega\setminus\Omega_{x})}\exp(-(n-2)p_{x}(z))\,dz

\displaystyle=O(\exp(-(p_{\min}/2^{d+2}d)nh^{d}))

\displaystyle=O(\frac{1}{n^{3}}),

with the last line following upon choosing
C_{1}\geq(p_{\min}/2^{d+2}d)^{-1/d}
in the definition of
h
.

On the other hand, if
z\in\Omega_{x}
then
B(z,\|z-x\|)\subset\Omega
.
Consequently, letting
\widetilde{p}_{x}(z):=p(x)\mu_{d}\|z-x\|^{d}
, it follows by
the Lipschitz property of
p
that

|p_{x}(z)-\widetilde{p}_{x}(z)|\leq\int_{B(z,\|z-x\|)}|p(z)-p(x)|\,dz\leq C\mu_{d}\|z-x\|^{d+1},

and

|\exp\bigl(-np_{x}(z)\bigr)-\exp\bigl(-n\widetilde{p}_{x}(z)\bigr)|\leq C\mu_{d}\|z-x\|^{d+1}n.

Additionally recall that
\exp(-np)\geq(1-p)^{n}\geq\exp(-np)(1-np^{2})

for any
|p|<1
. Combining these facts, we conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{L\cap\Omega_{x}}\Bigl(1-p_{x}(z)\Bigr)^{n}\,dz

\displaystyle=\int_{L\cap\Omega_{x}}\exp(-np_{x}(z))\bigl(1+O(np_{x}(z)^{2})\bigr)\,dz

(S.94)

\displaystyle=\int_{L\cap\Omega_{x}}\exp(-n\widetilde{p}_{x}(z))\Bigl(1+O(n\|z-x\|^{2d})+O(n\|z-x\|^{d+1})\Bigr)\,dz

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\int_{L\cap\Omega_{x}}\exp(-np(x)\mu_{d}\|x-z\|^{d})\,dz+O\Bigl(\frac{1}{n^{3}}+\frac{1}{n}1\bigl\{\|x-y\|\leq C_{2}(\log n/n)^{1/d}\bigr\}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{=}\int_{L}\exp\bigl(-np(x)\mu_{d}\|x-z\|^{d}\bigr)\,dz+O\Bigl(\frac{1}{n^{3}}+\frac{(\log n)^{2}}{n}1\bigl\{\|x-y\|\leq C_{2}(\log n/n)^{1/d}\bigr\}\Bigr).

We prove the last two equalities, which control the remainder terms, after
completing our analysis of the leading order term.

Step 2: Leading order term.

Let
r=\|x-y\|/2
. Due to rotational symmetry, we may as well take
x=re_{1},y=-re_{1}
, in which case the integral becomes

\displaystyle\int_{L}\exp\bigl(-np(x)\mu_{d}\|x-z\|^{d}\bigr)\,dz

\displaystyle=\int_{\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\exp\bigl(-np(x)\mu_{d}\|re_{1}-z\|^{d}\bigr)\,dz

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\exp\bigl(-np(x)\mu_{d}(r^{2}+\|z\|^{2})^{d/2}\bigr)\,dz,

with the latter equality following from the Pythagorean theorem. Converting
to polar coordinates, we see that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\exp\bigl(-np(x)\mu_{d}(r^{2}+\|z\|^{2})^{d/2}\bigr)\,dz

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-2}}\exp\bigl(-np(x)\mu_{d}(r^{2}+t^{2})^{d/2}\bigr)t^{d-2}\,d\theta\,dt

\displaystyle=\eta_{d-2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp\bigl(-np(x)\mu_{d}(r^{2}+t^{2})^{d/2}\bigr)t^{d-2}\,dt

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta_{d-2}}{(np(x))^{\frac{d-1}{d}}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp\Bigl(-\mu_{d}\bigl\{\bigl(r(np(x))^{1/d}\bigr)^{2}+s^{2}\bigr\}^{d/2}\Bigr)s^{d-2}\,ds,

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta_{d-2}}{(np(x))^{\frac{d-1}{d}}}K_{\mathrm{Vor}}\biggl(\frac{\|y-x\|}{\varepsilon_{(1)}}\biggr),

with the second to last equality following by substituting
s=t/(np(x))^{-1/d}
.

Controlling remainder terms.

We complete the proof of (
S.92
) by establishing
(i)

and
(ii)
in (
S.94
).

Proof of
(i)
.

Take
\varepsilon_{0}=(4\log n/\mu_{d}p_{\min}n)^{1/d}
, and note that if

\|z-x\|\geq\varepsilon_{0}
then
\exp\bigl(-\mu_{d}np(x)\|z-x\|^{d}\bigr)\leq\frac{1}{n^{4}}
. Recalling the definition of
\widetilde{p}_{x}(z)
,
we have

\displaystyle n\int_{L\cap\Omega_{x}}\exp\bigl(-n\widetilde{p}_{x}(z)\bigr)\|z-x\|^{d+1}\,dz

\displaystyle=n\int_{L\cap\Omega_{x}}\exp\bigl(-\mu_{d}np(x)\|z-x\|^{d}\bigr)\|z-x\|^{d+1}\,dz

(S.95)

\displaystyle\leq n\int_{L\cap B(x,\varepsilon_{0})}\exp\bigl(-\mu_{d}np_{\min}\|z-x\|^{d}\bigr)\|z-x\|^{d+1}\,dz+\frac{\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(L\cap\Omega)}{n^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq n\varepsilon_{0}^{d+1}\int_{L\cap B(x,\varepsilon_{0})}\exp\bigl(-\mu_{d}np_{\min}\|z-x\|^{d}\bigr)\,dz+\frac{\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(L\cap\Omega)}{n^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq n\varepsilon_{0}^{d+1}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(L\cap B(x,\varepsilon_{0}))+\frac{\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(L\cap\Omega)}{n^{3}}.

For any
x,y
we have
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(L\cap B(x,\varepsilon_{0}))\leq\mu_{d-1}\varepsilon_{0}^{d-1}
. If additionally
\|x-y\|/2>\varepsilon_{0}

then
L\cap B(x,\varepsilon_{0})=\emptyset
, and so
\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(L\cap B(x,\varepsilon_{0}))=0
. Compactly, these estimates can be written as

\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(L\cap B(x,\varepsilon_{0}))\leq\mu_{d-1}1\{\|x-y\|\leq 2\varepsilon_{0}\}\varepsilon_{0}^{d-1}.

Plugging this back into (
S.95
), we conclude that

\displaystyle n\int_{L\cap\Omega_{x}}\exp\bigl(-n\widetilde{p}_{x}(z)\bigr)\|z-x\|^{d+1}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq n\varepsilon_{0}^{2d}1\{\|x-y\|\leq 2\varepsilon_{0}\}+\frac{\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(L\cap\Omega)}{n^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigl(\frac{(\log n)^{2}}{n}1\{\|x-y\|\leq C_{2}(\log n/n)^{1/d}\}+\frac{1}{n^{3}}\Bigr),

for
C_{2}=2(4/(p_{\min}\mu_{d}))^{1/d}
. This is precisely the claim.

Proof of
(ii)
.

Recall the fact established previously, that if
z\in L\setminus\Omega_{x}

then
\|z-x\|\geq h/2
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\int_{L\setminus\Omega_{x}}\exp\bigl(-n\widetilde{p}_{x}(z)\bigr)\,dz

\displaystyle\leq\int_{L\setminus\Omega_{x}}\exp\bigl(-\mu_{d}np_{\min}\|z-x\|^{d}\bigr)\,dz

\displaystyle\leq\int_{L\setminus B(x,2)}\exp\bigl(-\mu_{d}np_{\min}\|z-x\|^{d}\bigr)\,dz+\int_{(L\cap B(x,2)\setminus\Omega_{x})}\exp\bigl(-\mu_{d}p_{\min}n(h/2)^{d}\bigr)\,dz

\displaystyle\leq\int_{L\setminus B(x,2)}\exp\bigl(-\mu_{d}np_{\min}\|z-x\|^{d}\bigr)\,dz+\frac{\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(L\cap B(x,2))}{n^{3}},

with the last inequality following upon choosing
C_{1}\geq 2/(\mu_{d}p_{\min})^{1/d}
in the definition of
h
. The remaining integral is
exponentially small in
